ParlaMint-AT_1997-01-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00058_d7e2497	Thank you, Mr President. – Deputies! I can tell you that at the last Council of Ministers meeting before Christmas, on 17 November. In December 1996, a government proposal for a new plant protection legislation was adopted. With regard to the points you have raised here in particular, this Plant Protection Act provides for the government template forwarded to Parliament that, in accordance with § 12/1, mutual recognition of plant protection products authorisations granted by individual EU countries is regulated in the manner set out in Article 10 of the Plant Protection Products Directive. In other words, there will be an accelerated procedure in Austria if, first, it is an active substance which has already been subject to a so-called Community active substance assessment in the European Union – it is a very modern procedure, which is also carried out by Austria; Active substances are also evaluated by our experts on behalf of the European Union, and it can be assumed that, as has been said, this active substance assessment will be carried out in accordance with the latest criteria or has been implemented since 1996 – and that, secondly, the environmental conditions of the EU country in which the authorisation is made and Austria are comparable. § 12/2 of this Government template provides that, under the condition of an existing administrative agreement, for example with the Federal Republic of Germany, products which have been approved in Austria for some time could only be subject to a formal authorisation procedure. But, as has been said, this requires a mutual administrative agreement, which will undoubtedly have to take account of those criteria and which, according to § 12/1, are standardised, namely very similar environmental conditions, climatic conditions, in order to be able to impose the conditions of approval at the same level.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-01-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00058_d7e2691	Mr. Federal Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-01-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00058_d7e2706	Mr Schrefel. — (DE) Mr President, ladies and gentlemen. I can tell you that, even before Austria's accession to the EU, there have been considerable reductions in the use of plant protection products, our agricultural policy has clearly achieved great success. I can tell you that in the five-year period - from 1991 to 1995 - the use of plant protection products has been reduced by around 25 percent. The exact figures: from 4 487 tons to only 3 404 tonnes. This is a uniquely low value across Europe for the hectares. It can be assumed that there has always been, or at least, a very ecological agricultural policy in this country in recent years. The specific impact of the ÖPUL in the first year of Austria's membership in the European Union is that the use of plant protection products in 1995 fell by 6 percent, that fungicide use fell by 10 percent, insecticide use by 10 percent, and the use of growth regulators by 58 percent. In the first year of membership of the European Union, the line was therefore not only maintained, but even strengthened. That is why agriculture minister Willi Molterer tells me that in the next five years – by 1999 – we will be able to expect a further reduction in the use of plant protection products of around 20 percent.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-01-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00058_d7e3110	Mr. Federal Minister! What is the level of radioactive pollution on the environment of Austria due to the Chernobyl disaster?
ParlaMint-AT_1997-01-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00058_d7e3125	Mr. Federal Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-01-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00058_d7e3140	Unfortunately, higher than all of us accept. Austria, which few know, was the country most affected by the radioactive fall-out to Chernobyl in April and May 1986, with the exception of the direct region around Kiev and Minsk. Belarus has been more affected than Ukraine – due to the unfavourable weather and wind conditions. In Austria we have an average floor load of 16 kilocecquerels per square meter. Unfortunately, that's not little. We even have top values of over 100 Kilobecquerel in Carinthian-Steirian mountain regions that were quite affected by the rain at that time. We will also have to live with this burden for centuries because of the half-life of the corresponding radioactive isotope, which is exactly 300 years, even if, of course, it decreases continuously within these 300 years. However, I can say that the entry of all these forests, the eating of berries, sponges and the like is more harmful to Austrian consumers, because it can be assumed that the enjoyment of these sponges that grow there is not the only food supply over a longer period of time. So there are no concerns here. This is certain, and it is well-tested, but it remains to be noted that eleven years after Chernobyl, we must not only feel this accident, which should never have happened, but that we in Austria will be able to measure the radiation pollution from the consequences of Chernobyl for almost 300 years.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-01-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00058_d7e3252	Federal Minister! They have recently presented a family management model that will bring 12 billion schilling to the children or their parents. What were Their reflections on this, or their basics? [[/PAKT/VG/XX/M/M_00089/index.shtml -- 89/M]] What considerations are the basis for the family tax model presented by you, which is intended to bring about 12 billion schilling to the children or the parents?
ParlaMint-AT_1997-01-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00058_d7e3280	Mr. Federal Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-01-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00058_d7e3295	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) I'll try to explain this shortly. The most important consideration of this family tax concept is that on the one hand, several children's families on the one hand and single parents – in 90 percent of cases – on the other hand cannot participate in the development of prosperity in Austria, as would be desirable. It is significant, for example, that, although only a third of the population in that country has less than half of the average income of all the other Austrians available, single earnings families with two, three, four children have far higher percentages, up to 70 percent, below this limit, which is referred to by some as poverty limits, and I strongly point out that poverty is very relative here. But after all, this clearly shows that there is a motive here to use multiple children's families on the one hand and single parents' families on the other hand more strongly than before. This model fully meets these requirements. It brings families up to 20,000 S – after taxes – per year, for example, a single earner family with three children. It brings low-income families, Mrs Mertel, more than high-income families, because 20 000 S for a low-income family means more than for a stronger income family. It continues to be based on the principle of individual taxation, and above all it must be assumed and hoped that this would also be a constitutional solution, ladies and gentlemen.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-01-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00058_d7e3362	(Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) The proposed increases in the amount of children's deposits by 400 S, which constitute the basic structure of this family tax regime, are intended to be paid to families and mothers who cannot use this tax amount for tax reasons as a negative tax – as it has always been. ParlaMint-AT_1997-01-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00058_d7e5253	Mr President. Federal Minister! High house! With the change in the rental rights according to the government template, the condition ended with the 3rd. The law on the right of residence was introduced. It is now – and I believe this to be the most important change, which we are in principle in favour of – to extend the time-limitation possibilities. Ladies and gentlemen! This is a repair law that has become necessary to keep the famous and famous city nomadism in the future. It is, as has been said, a repair law, but a reform is not. I want to prevent false expectations. It is therefore not a reform because in this case it should have been much more involved. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] In my opinion, the time limits now provided for rental conditions of at least three and a maximum of ten years with three interest rates are very complicated. However, we can certainly discuss this because the original intention of the government parties that negotiated it was that different incentives should be created for the landlord depending on the length of the rental relationship. I think it is necessary to discuss this, and we believe, in the sense of our four-sided amendment, that at least in the near future, a uniform discount rate of 20 percent can be achieved. For us, however, this is not a "war of faith", we are willing to discuss it. For the sake of increased legal certainty, we believe that the right of requisition of the landlord is properly regulated. This is the main problem: In Austria, we have about the most complicated rental right of all Europe. Therefore, it would be urgent to unbundle in order to give greater legal certainty to all parties involved. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] On the other hand, ladies and gentlemen, if the Government's proposal is to be adopted, I fear that it will come to an end. I would like to remind you that this legal uncertainty will make it difficult for us to deal with it. Another point in our amendment concerns a system or a scheme which is therefore possible to derogate from the previous indicative value systems even in the case of old houses. This applies to cases where old houses are in a very good condition following a general renovation and this condition is certified by an expert opinion which must not be older than ten years. In such cases, it should be possible for the landlord to request an adequate rent in the sense of an extension of the accommodation offer. The fifth point will be Mr Krüger and Mr Ofner. A number of further amendments concern special problems such as the question of remuneration, which we believe are problematic in terms of fixing the surplus revenue and so on. Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to point out a very essential point that should have been taken into account in a genuine reform. I understand that this is a hot iron, and that there is a strong exchange between the government parties, so that it is preferable not to raise this question. It concerns, in my view, the reform of the housing law on non-profit-making. An amendment to § 14 para. 7, in which it is a matter of securing a right of co-ownership for the tenants of cooperative dwellings. In contrast to the acquisition of a regular housing property which already exists or is registered at the beginning, this would, according to our request, be foreseen at the end of the disbursement. Ladies and gentlemen! With all the clarity, I must say that the fact of dealing with cooperative apartments is a twirl of our legal system. The practice seems to be to reduce non-profit housing to a rental right, rather than to secure a co-ownership for the inhabitants of such apartments. What's going on in practice could be described with the word "preflection of false facts" or with the modern keyword "label-labelling" Ladies and gentlemen! The central point in § 14 para. 7 of the housing law of public utility concerns the so-called end-of-life profit, which Werner Faymann likes to destroy with the term "Körberlgeld". Why is this the central criticism? – I believe that the tenants of cooperative apartments are not at all quite aware of what this "carbage money" is and that this semantic term is used to cover up. Ladies and gentlemen! To get it to the point: The demand of the liberals is clear and unmistakable. We demand that, after full funding, housing cooperatives automatically transfer property rights without asking them to cash out for a second time. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] After funding, only the conservation contributions have to be made, otherwise nothing else, ladies and gentlemen. If now the red and black coalition in Vienna under the pressure of the Vienna Freemans says that you have to think about taking cooperatives into duty and reducing interest on category A or otherwise suspending the promotion of property, I have to say: But that can only be a first step. It is not enough because the proceeds persist. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] Ladies and gentlemen! To keep in mind the scope of this measure, I call you a few numbers in all hurry. The speech is currently of 60 000 previously financed cooperatives, who, cautiously reckoned, have a traffic value of 25 billion schilling. In 18 years, in 2015, 300,000 homes will be financed, with a value of 125 billion schilling on today's basis. This is the value of real property that will keep the cooperative tenants away. In the case of "carrier money", the result is the amount of current 1.1 billion schilling annually. This will increase to 5.5 billion schilling by 2015. In these 18 years, co-operative tenants will retain a total of 55.8 billion schillings from "Körberlgelgelgeld" which are incorporated into the building land reserve, into the property reserve of the cooperatives. In this way, ladies and gentlemen, cooperatives are getting richer and poorer and poorer. That cannot be the meaning of a law of public utility! [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] It has been shown that property in the final effect is cheaper than rent, which can be determined by considering this legal regulation. Ladies and gentlemen! With all clarity, I would also like to say: There is not only a right to dwelling, but also a right to property, a property which is granted to cooperatives as real property, and not a property which benefits the benefit of the common good. On the other hand, my criticism is directed. [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] Ladies and gentlemen! This must be demanded with all vehemence. If we make pure table in housing, we create clear categories: here rent, there cooperative housing system with property and on the other hand property! Only by this can the Party Book and Prunden economy be dismantled, and the credibility of politics would truly be a good service!

ParlaMint-AT_1997-01-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00058_d7e6046	Ladies and gentlemen! Mr. Federal Minister! It was really interesting to listen to colleagues Barmüller, and I said that there are no fears to discuss this issue seriously here in the House and with the Liberals. Only: I do not believe one thing: that the results can then be achieved so easily and so easily and as quickly as possible through this discussion, because the topic of living – Mr Barmüller, you know just as well as I do – is one of the most sensitive social policy areas, and the fair and manageable distribution of housing according to the individual needs and demands of people requires a constant critical consideration, and therefore we must always work on these legal instruments. At the same time, however, it must be noted that political views – and we have already heard from my previous speakers – are very different: On the one hand, the representatives of the homeowners – and my previous speaker has argued in this direction – are not necessarily the most pressing concern for the protection of tenants and consumers, and on the other hand the representatives of the tenants and those looking for housing. Ladies and gentlemen! Social democracy has always clearly taken a position in favour of tenants and those seeking housing, especially since, due to the situation on the housing market, these would often be in a much worse position without adequate legal protection. As already pointed out, the different attitudes on this subject naturally force us to compromise; Mr Schwimmer said that too. There are quite simply compromises, and one has to negotiate them, and one has to get through them, even if one does not love the compromise. However, we have always stressed that this is the third. The law on housing rights also constitutes a compromise for us and in no way the full implementation of social-democratic housing programmes. However, we have always pointed out those points of the compromise that are less satisfactory from our point of view. One of them is certainly in the field of deadlines that we are discussing today. ==References== Indeed, the law on housing rights has effect from 1 January 1994. In this area, a new general temporary status, the so-called three-year contract, was created on March 14, 1994. Of course, one can critically judge what has already happened here today, but this three-year contract – and this should be made clear again here again – has led to mass quantifications, for example in agglomerations such as in Vienna, to the massively practised half-yearly and annual contracts, which one seems to no longer wish to remember in conservative circles, which have led to a clear improvement of the tenants concerned. As from March 1997, the first one is now on the 3rd. Three-year lease contracts – and it was a difficult compromise to achieve these three years – have been established and, in the current legal situation, only a termination of the lease or an indefinite renewal would be possible, but no longer possible, the further compromise has now been drawn up. It is important to note that, of course, the Social Democrats, as a tenant protection party, continue to enter into a rule contract for the indefinite lease contract. That's what we need to discuss! If we really do all this, the question arises whether we can then even call this contract a rule contract, especially since it offers the highest level of security for the tenant – that is, this rule contract. Then: The tenant can, if he wants, take off at any time. Then why do we make a term? - Because the landlord wants him out sometime. That is the problem! At the same time, it must be noted that many landlords are obviously not willing to convert existing three-year contracts into indefinite contracts. They could have done that too! If we had not made a regulation, the alternative would have been: The "love", "braven" landlords simply turn the three-year contracts into indefinite contracts, and we had no problems at all. But that's not happening! And in this tension relationship, ladies and gentlemen, it was therefore important to find suitable solutions for all sides. Ladies and gentlemen! In Vienna, for example, about 70 000 rental contracts are currently being concluded annually, about 10 percent of which are temporary. It should already be concluded from this figure that there is a great deal of thought and sensitivity to this issue. I am convinced that the proposed modifications in the field of time limits are in line with both the objective of increased tenants' kindness and the increased flexibility of time-limits. Main rental apartments and leased older properties, such as in buildings with a building permit before the 9th century. May 1945, may now be rented in writing for a period of three to ten years. Thus, the minimum duration is three years, the maximum duration – even if the contract extension is combined – is ten years. An exception exists only with regard to the older dwellings mentioned above, where we deliberately do not have a time limit for the original or extended duration of the temporary contract. Due to this more flexible handling of the time limit, tenants whose landlords refuse to extend a three-year contract must now no longer withdraw from the apartment, but can stay in the apartment. That was the goal of this novel. In addition to written deadlines or extensions, a one-off non-written extension may also take place by one year; Mr Schwimmer has already pointed this out. Ladies and gentlemen! Further significant innovations for the tenant are that, even if the contract has been concluded for three years or more, the tenant already has the right after the end of a year – even with a ten-year contract! – to announce the rental contract in compliance with a three-month period. The possibility of recovery of excessive interest on rents – and this has already been pointed out; this was a special demand from my group – will be extended to the duration of the term plus half a year, i.e. to a maximum of ten and a half years, for the entire duration, not only for a maximum of three years, as is usual under commercial law. This will ensure, ladies and gentlemen, that tenants can actually use their rights and not give up because they still hope to be transformed into an indefinite contract. A further incentive to choose longer term contracts or contracts which are the same for an indefinite period of time lies in the agreed rent rates, and the motto is that: The shorter the contract duration, the less favourable to the landlord. We have set percentages of 10 to 30 percent according to the different times that one can see from the law. We have also found it useful that a temporary contract should be converted into an indefinite contract by means of a simple agreement between the landlord and the tenant, and the landlord should then have the right to receive a refund of the amount that has been paid in the limited period of time by the tenant, who can thus buy an unlimited rental contract. I think that's good. However, it would be useful for the tenant to have the rent rate checked once, so that his entire rental rate fixed for an indefinite rental period is also appropriate. For existing three-year contracts, this means an extension of at least three and a maximum of seven years, but with those rent-rate reductions which also result in new rental contracts. It is also a unique, not written, for example silent extension possible – that has already been said by Mr Schwimmer – and I really think this is very, very good. Ladies and gentlemen! I believe that the law which we are discussing here today and which makes our decision can, with this novice, go a substantial step forward towards further housing security. I would like to say once again: In principle, temporary contracts are not the goal of the Social Democratic Group. But in the situation in which we are now, the compromise in the form we have now concluded is acceptable. Allow me, ladies and gentlemen, to say thank you very much to the Federal Minister and his officials for the really constructive and good cooperation. I hope and am actually sure that this will be the case in the future. - Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-01-23-020-XX-NRSITZ-00058_d7e6352	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! The understanding of the new rules of this government template is facilitated by their consideration in the historical context. Of the three priorities, the time-limits and the settlement rules in "Mixed Houses" have their roots in the 3rd century. The law on housing rights or the circumstances of its law, the new regulation relating to the rental interest reserve in the absence of the previous tax regime by the Structural Adjustment Act 1996. Although the law in force in 1981 has been adopted by the Rent Act, that the lease contract - which is only limited by the landlord - should be the case for an indefinite period of time, it has, however, allowed temporary leases up to a maximum of half a year to be completely excluded from its scope and for a maximum period of one year. In practice, especially the half-year contracts had led to significant problems and disadvantages for socially weaker tenants and had also been the starting point for many circumvention attempts. Its removal was therefore one of the most important concerns of the 3rd century. Law on housing rights. At the same time, instead of the one-year contract, the possibility of concluding a housing lease contract was created for exactly three years without a temporary extension. This rigid legal requirement prevents, on the one hand, individual choice of contract duration corresponding to the needs of the Contracting Parties and, on the other hand, presents the landlord with the alternative, after the end of the three years, to either insist on the tenant's extract or, by an unlimited rental relationship, to lose the disposition over the rental object indefinitely. This seems to have been done in the expectation that the new three-year contracts would not necessarily be used as a result of the proposed deduction arrangements in practice. In fact, many, certainly some of the zig thousands of such lease contracts have been concluded, which will gradually expire from March this year. Since many landlords are not willing to establish an unlimited rental relationship by leaving the tenant in the apartment, without a legislative intervention, most of the temporary tenants would be required to extract from their apartment and to search for a new rental property, and they would often only receive temporary rentable accommodation. However, one of some and also of my predecessors, only on occasion-related law repair – for example, by creating only an extension of existing temporary rental contracts – would not in truth eliminate the acute problem in Bälde, but would only delay it for some time. On the other hand, it seemed more useful to me when the step towards temporary rental contracts was taken to initiate a fundamental, modern renewal of the time limit in the rental right. I am convinced that the proposed path of flexibilising the time-limits for the contract within time limits and limits is a reasonable one. A balance between the tenants' needs for secure housing – at least for manageable periods of time – on the one hand, and the legitimate interests of the landlords at a medium to longer-term re-availability of their apartments, on the other, and offers a future-oriented perspective on the other. The basic understanding inherent in the rental law since its creation, according to which the lease is to be the case for an indefinite period, remains true as a principle and is to be ensured by a kind of bonus malus system, for example by means of the provisions on the time-limits, and I am voting for them, Mr Mag. Firlinger prefers a single discount scheme because it provides incentives within the maximum time limit to a leased leased in the interest of the tenant as long as possible, or, for example, by the provisions on the leased interest-repayment offered to the lessor in case of conversion into an indefinite rental relationship. In order to ensure the future protection of tenants, the liberalized time-limits are also opposed to a number of arrangements to ensure the legal position of tenants, e.g. the extension of the time limits for the court's rental interest rate check and the recovery of overpaid rental interest payments, the option of the tenant to extend the rental relationship by one year, the adapted possibilities for early termination of the contract by the tenant, who can use the market conditions in his favour, and a few more. In order to avoid circumventions, all of these provisions apply also to residences in old houses, except for the maximum permitted total term of ten years, which will not apply in future to residences in old houses or to residences in younger houses. As far as the billing rules are concerned, very detailed expert advice has shown that their comprehensive harmonisation in the three major housing law laws, which is referred to by the national council resolution at that time, is not appropriate and, in part, impossible, therefore it was concentrated on those areas in which, according to the experience of practice, this is necessary, namely, the so-called mixed houses in which, in addition to newly established housing ownership, there are still earlier major rental or use conditions. The harmonisation model developed for this purpose is based primarily on an agreement between the parties concerned and, second, on the key to lease conditions. However, all three types of housing law will in future be able to take account of the relevant ÖNORM on the billing in recent years and judicial decisions on applications for the verification of operating costs will apply not only to applicants but to all tenants. The new regulation relating to the rent-rate reserve is linked to the elimination of the tax advantages for it by the Structural Adjustment Act 1996 and the national Council resolution at that time which I was asked to bring about a harmonisation of the tax and residential provisions on the rental-rate reserve. I do not know that I would have preferred harmonisation with changes in tax law to the adaptation of the rental law now provided for in the proposal for a law. However, this could not at least be realized for fiscal policy reasons. Thus, it remained only the mean-law variant to provide for an additional expenditure position to take into account the tax burden for the calculation of the rental interest-rate reserve. This would be linked to a number of far-reaching complications, but also to inadequacies, so that only a flat-rate arrangement could be applied by a generally applicable percentage. It is now in the essence of such a package that it cannot bring full justice to all possible cases. However, the bill has tried to propose an acceptable solution in the light of the present conditions. However, I think that further work on this point within the meaning of the motion for a resolution which the coalition parties seem to have proposed today makes sense, and I would also particularly welcome the tax relief of the temporary contracts and their extension in the Committee on Justice. In conclusion, I believe that the new rental rights novels presented on urgent issues of housing law are ready for appropriate, appropriate and, to a large extent, forward-looking answers. However, in the coming years, it will be possible to observe exactly what the actual impact of the new regulations will be. However, the development of housing law in terms of its economic and social dimension is a permanent discussion process, which will certainly involve the further proposals and suggestions made today by the Federal Ministry of Justice. - Thank you many times.

It is highly interesting to find out who is appealing to the stock company: Mr Seiler wants her, the SPÖ wants her, the Court of Auditors wants her, the ÖVP wants her when her wishes are fulfilled – the opposition does not want her. I believe that it is primarily a matter of meeting the various needs: The management wants to maintain the Moloch ORF in the current execution – that would be guaranteed by the AG. The Court of Auditors wishes to see business structures that are much more flexible, management responsibilities and other things that would be absolutely welcome. The ÖVP wishes above all for the proportional division – especially expressed by the head of state Schausberger –: 50 percent country, 50 percent federal government. I personally believe, however, that the main reason for a AG's claim for the ORF lies in the future infinancing of the ORF. I would like to quote briefly from the report, although there is little time available to me: "In order to continue the ORF in the current range of services, both the financing by means of broadcasting fees and as much advertising revenue as possible are necessary. A significant decrease in advertising revenue – and this is safe in the future – "may have been required in the longer term by the RH either rising fees or a public hand injection." This means that, in addition to the fees, taxpayers will also be required to receive the ORF in the current execution. And I believe that this is the background to the demand for a stock company, because in the case of federal ownership and state ownership 50 : 50 the ownership liability can be beaten. – We act against such financing of the ORF, and it will not happen as long as we have something to say! [[Congratulations on the liberals.]] I think, rather, that we must finally eliminate the embarrassment of the ORF monopoly. The rule of law in Strasbourg took place three years ago, and for three years Austria has been embarrassed to maintain a monopoly against human rights. There is a private TV law to make clear. One has to discuss publicly and widely what the public service mandate in the future has to look like, it derives from the necessary structures and tasks of the ORF, and only then can one debate about the legal form and funding of the ORF. It is therefore all the more important to shape this company in terms of its future in such a way that it can be successful even in the face of greater challenges. The Austrian Federal Government's Convention on Labour states that for the better possible solution for the tasks of the future of the ORF to a capital company, preferably to be converted into a stock company. If we consider that Austria has nine federal states and the federal government cannot have less supervisory councils than the countries, and if, in accordance with the Constitutional Labour Act, we also count the works councils, then this will be a body which may be larger than today's board of trustees and cannot decide more quickly at all. However, we now know from practice that the distributed lawyers are at times skeptical of the wishes and the review of the public prosecutor's office, and if nothing changes and if doubts persist, it will probably be in the interest of the Republic of Austria to eliminate this control deficit by creating clarity through a positive standardisation. Ladies and gentlemen! One point has not yet been raised, which, in my opinion, is also very important, so that the People's Procuratorate, which already has a great acceptance in the population, still receives a greater acceptance and even greater understanding and trust by the population: I address the responsibility of the audited authority. It is standardised by law that the authority has a comprehensive obligation to support it. At the same time, the People's Procuratorate also complains about the fact that certain authorities are only very hesitant in their replies and that the file template and the answer often take place for many months. We also know that, just as it is in the attorney's profession, every summation of an authority or court is pushed into the shoes of the lawyer, without he being able to do anything for it, is also in the population. If a complaint is filed to the public prosecutor's office and the authority fails to respond, it will ultimately be charged with the public prosecutor's image and confidence towards the public prosecutor's office. The consideration of the public prosecutor's office is to lay down the obligation to reply within a certain period of time, and the four-week period proposed by the Public Prosecutor's Office is safe to join. Mrs Frieser rightly referred to the fact that, if she wishes, the People's Procuratorate should also be heard in the legislative process. In the specific case, the proposal was made that the public prosecutorship should be entitled but not obliged to take part in committee meetings of the House. This participation is appropriate: The public prosecutorship could bring in its experience in the enforcement of the laws and their weaknesses, and this can only be beneficial in the sense of understandable and easily manageable and meaningful laws. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen! Finally, I join the birthday wishes of the People's Procuratorate – we are celebrating the 20th anniversary this year. I would like to congratulate you on giving the people of the People's Procuratorate great confidence, and as a representative of the Free Opposition, I can announce our full support when it comes to supporting the improvement of examination competence.

The Court of Auditors report on the State Police for the review period October to December 1994 is now under discussion here in this House. The tasks of the State Police were carried out by Group C, which is under the responsibility of the Directorate-General for Public Security at the Ministry of the Interior. The Group C, in turn, consists of three departments: Division II/6, Human and Object Protection, Division II/7, State Protection, and Special Unit EBT, the Action Group to combat terrorism. The Court of Auditors, in its review, noted that the operation between the individual departments and the special unit's units should be better coordinated so that, first, it cannot happen in the future that the right hand does not know what the left hand is doing, and secondly, that the same work should not be carried out several times. It is therefore necessary, as recommended by the Court of Auditors, to coordinate the areas of activity in a organisational manner and to avoid the carrying out of tasks. 22 Divisions for State, Person and Object Protection are subordinated to Group C. Two thirds of these were occupied by two to ten officials, who also had to perform duties for other departments. In its report, the Court recommended reducing the number of subordinate bodies in order to avoid fragmentation of forces. Since both a division and a special unit of Group D and a special unit of Group C were responsible for combating the various forms of organised crime, the Court recommended a merger of both groups for joint planning and cooperation. The Federal Minister for the Interior, however, stated at a meeting of the Court of Auditors that it would not be useful to bring these groups together, since there is a clear division of tasks. – A regular exchange of information and better cooperation between the prosecutor and the state police following the announcements or feedback made, have also been missed by the Court of Auditors. A particular problem was found by the Court of Auditors during the overtime. On average, 100 hours of overtime per month were performed without a record of services. According to the Minister's information, the number of overtime has been reduced by introducing the daily report and amending the mandate. Some points were still highlighted by the Court of Auditors and considered worthy of improvement, such as the conversion of the Journal Service, the management of the Special Unit, in which the Court of Auditors believes that too often new inexperienced officials were entrusted with executive functions, the risk allowance, the administrative activity, the foreign union, the establishment plan, the adjustment of which the Court of Auditors wished, since all officials working in the executive service were at the expense of the Federal Police and the Federal Army. High house! It is, I think, far more important to talk about the future from the problems that have been shown not to beat political change. The tasks that will still be assigned to the State Police will certainly not be easier. I have expressed my respect for every official who is willing to stand up for the security of this country – and sometimes also for his health and his life for it. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] These people belong, I believe, to support, and politicians and we politicians have the task of making available to them all the possible means they need to combat crime. The Minister of the Interior has the task of creating basic organisational conditions. This has happened in part, but still worth improving – like much in the world. It is up to us here in this House to monitor, control and make proposals for improvement those measures which the Minister of the Interior sets out. In some stocks, fertility has decreased from six to nine calves to under three calves. In milk, the number of cells has increased from less than 100,000 in many farms, although they have worked properly, to more than 100,000 cells. In this debate, however, we must note that there are also counter-arguments. Of course it's like this: The younger you are, the less attention you give to traffic. We must find ways of perhaps reducing the age limit without endangering road safety. However, I believe that this path must be found - without the addition to this request that this should apply only to certain routes or to a specific purpose, because either someone is mature to take part in the traffic with a vehicle or he is not. It can't be that you say in one direction someone can drive with the moped, but not in the other direction, because that can't be the one. I therefore believe that we should discuss this in peace. We should consider what we can do in the course of training in order to achieve a certain degree of security, as far as possible, so that we may be able to reduce the age limit, but we have the certainty that this does not create additional risks and that the young people concerned are still at risk. However, this discussion should also be used to eliminate a traffic jam at the KFG-Novelle. I think there is a need for action here. We need to talk about what improved training in the transport sector as a whole should look like. This should no longer be stopped or blocked by the point driver's license in any form. I think the point guide ticket is not the change and rise in Austria. We should take a few steps forward in this area, apart from the point guide ticket, and in the area of reducing the age for mopeding, we should come to a solution in the interest of the youth of Austria. Thank you.

As far as this proposal is concerned, we Liberals have the feeling that there is a certain relationship between individual agenda items and, above all, the principles that the EIA represents here. It cannot be so much from north to south in the one direction 5 kilometers and this route is retourable, but not from east to west or from west to east. That's kind of not agreeable, you'll admit it to me. It is therefore simply a question of: Is it right to allow people 15 years old to drive Mofa everywhere? It is not to be seen why they should only drive on a certain route, and in practice it will be so that people do not stick to it. You have to see that clearly, too. [[Abg. Fink: We didn't want that anyway! Do they stick to it today?]] Deputies! There's something important to me: In fact, you have no concern that you can cycle much earlier. And if I take part on a bicycle in the traffic, then if I'm strong enough, I'm allowed to "bubble" like the devil, that can be sausages, uphill or downhill, but I can't do that with the Mofa. May. Kukacka: Yes, let's lower it!]] You're saying this with a shoulder tick: Let's lower it! Kukacka: So that the concerned persons too can agree!]] This can then be discussed in the Committee, Mr Deputies.
